Last it types `featherfall --seed 7` and plays it with QMP keys, checking screendump pixels at the game's own layout (the well 10x20 cells of 32 pixels centered on 1024x768): the empty well (well border, left panel border, background, an empty cell, a grid line), `left` held for 0.8 s and `space` leaving a piece color in the bottom row's second cell (every piece moved to the left wall fills it), `p` showing the pause box (box color, accent letters, the covered cell hiding the piece), `p` resuming, Alt+F2 showing a text console and Alt+F1 the pause box again (switching away pauses, and the whole screen must be presented again), rounds of five `space` presses until the game-over screen shows the initials slot, `a`, `b`, `c` in the three slots, `ret` showing the table with the first row highlighted, `esc` returning the text console and prompt, and `cat /home/.featherfall` printing `ABC <score>`. Pass it with `GRAPHICS_TEST=ON ./build.sh test --arch x86_64` or `--arch aarch64` (matrix cases 47 and 48). `DUNGEON_TEST=ON` (`--expect-dungeon`, `tools/smoke/cases/dungeon.py`, matrix cases 49 and 50) types `wickdeep --selftest`, `wickdeep --demo --seed 1` and `--seed 2` and requires `wickdeep: selftest ok` and the two exact demo lines (the same on both architectures), then saves a seed 1 demo at turn 500 to `/tmp/w.sav`, requires `--check-save` to say `save ok depth 2 turn 500`, appends a byte with `echo x >> /tmp/w.sav` and requires `save rejected length`; then it plays the game with QMP keys and `screendump` (helpers in `tools/smoke/cases/screen.py`, shared with `GRAPHICS_TEST`; layout constants at the top of `dungeon.py`, `HOME=/home`): `wickdeep --seed 7` must show the panel's border and fill, the margin, the heart icon, the wick-keeper's glow colour on the floor, lit floor and at least 80% of the floor still black; `i` opens the pack and Esc closes it (the floor equals the first dump again), `h` and a key the same for the help; 20 arrow presses change the floor; Alt+F2 shows the text console and Alt+F1 brings back a screen identical to the one before; Esc returns the prompt and `--check-save` says `save ok depth 1 turn N`; `wickdeep` again shows the floor from before Esc exactly; after `echo x >> /home/.wickdeep-save` a new `wickdeep --seed 7` shows the first seed-7 floor and the message about the save set aside, and `/home/.wickdeep-save.bad` exists; last a save copied from the turn-500 demo is resumed, its pack lists items with the first row highlighted, Down moves the highlight, Enter and `d` change the box, and Ctrl+C returns the prompt with a save of a later turn. The titles of the pack and the help are compared pixel for pixel with `ffnt.py` renderings of `sans-32`, and (as in `GRAPHICS`, whose left-panel numbers are checked cell by cell against `mono-32`) the case ends with `umount /system`, which hides the fonts (the initramfs is the built-in mount that holds them) and the rescue programs: the game must print `cannot load the font ...`, draw with the console font (`FEATHERFALL` or the pack title pixel for pixel in `mono-16`) and exit. The mode takes about 145 s on x86-64 and 105 s on AArch64 (`DUNGEON_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` 300). `FONTS_TEST=ON` (`--expect-fonts`, `tools/smoke/cases/fonts.py`, matrix cases 51 and 52, `FONTS_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` 120) types `cat /system/fonts/sample.txt` and, through QMP `screendump`, compares the sample's rows on the console grid (cells of 8x16 pixels, one cell of padding, row 0 the status bar) with cells that `tools/fonts/ffnt.py` renders from the committed `mono-16.ffnt`, clipped to the cell and blended over black as the kernel does; the sample (`tools/fonts/sample.txt`, kept equal to `initramfs/fonts/sample.txt` by `check.py`) has accented Latin, Greek, Cyrillic, arrows, punctuation, blocks, box drawing, a C1 line that must show no cells and a line of invalid bytes that must show U+FFFD cells (14). The console decodes UTF-8 in `Term` (one code point per cell, invalid bytes U+FFFD per maximal subpart, C1 dropped) and draws `mono-16` anti-aliased; `Terminal::Write` passes bytes above 0x7F to it. It then types `echo` of accented text and box drawing over serial (checked on screen and in the session), runs `fontview --check` on every file in `/system/fonts` (the glyph count and line height must equal what `ffnt.py` reads), on a copy of `sans-16.ffnt` with a byte appended (`font rejected length`) and on a missing file (`font unreadable`), and last `fontview --card`: once it prints `card drawn` the whole screendump must equal the 1024x768 image `CardReference` in `fonts.py` composes from `ffnt.py` renderings of the committed files (the layout is written out in both places, not shared); Esc returns the prompt. To change a font, regenerate it as described under "Graphics library"; `FONTS`, `fonts.files` and `fonts.reproducible` then tell if anything else must follow.

To run only one architecture's panic check, use `PANIC_TEST=ON`; this intentionally panics during startup, and the smoke test passes only when it observes the expected panic record:

```sh
PANIC_TEST=ON ./build.sh test --arch x86_64
PANIC_TEST=ON QEMU_EFI=/path/to/AAVMF_CODE.fd ./build.sh test --arch aarch64
```

## AArch64 Target

The cross-toolchain builder installs under `~/.local` by default and does not need `sudo`. It adds `~/.local/bin` to `PATH` when building. Build the AArch64 cross-compiler, then select that target for each build action:

```sh
./toolchains/build_toolchain.sh --binutils --gcc --arch aarch64-elf
ARCH=aarch64 ./build.sh build
ARCH=aarch64 ./build.sh iso
QEMU_EFI=/path/to/AAVMF_CODE.fd ARCH=aarch64 ./build.sh test
```

The AArch64 ISO is UEFI-only and uses Limine's `BOOTAA64.EFI`. QEMU runs with the `virt` machine. Interactive runs let the guest restart and power off, so the shell's `reboot` boots again and `shutdown` closes QEMU; set `QEMU_NO_REBOOT=1` to make a reset exit QEMU instead, which helps catch a triple fault during boot. The window scales the guest's 1024×768 screen with its size (`-display cocoa,zoom-to-fit=on` on macOS, GTK elsewhere), since pixel for pixel it is small on a Retina display; resize the window or use View → Zoom To Fit. Set `QEMU_ZOOM=0` for the default display at its native size, as on a QEMU built without Cocoa or GTK. They copy serial output to the launching terminal and to `build/<arch>/serial.log` as it arrives, removing the screen-clearing control sequences the firmware and Limine send on every boot. The log covers the whole session, including the boots after a `reboot`, and each new run starts a fresh log. Set `QEMU_EFI` to an AArch64 UEFI firmware image if the script cannot find one in a common system location.

On macOS, the toolchain builder automatically selects Apple Clang, the active Xcode SDK, GNU89 host flags, and system zlib for Binutils. Build each stage separately:

```sh
./toolchains/build_toolchain.sh --binutils --arch aarch64-elf
./toolchains/build_toolchain.sh --gcc --arch aarch64-elf
```

The builder accepts `HOST_CC`, `HOST_CFLAGS`, and `SDKROOT` overrides if needed.
